. After-all, if there were some we could add, then we could keep adding those event types to the sequence until all pairs from different protocols are interfacing.
Since there are no elements in
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}_{i+1}
that can be added, this means there is no chain of pairwise non-concurrent event types in
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}_{i+1}
that starts with
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i}
and ends in
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i+1}
.
Thus, we can rearrange the path (by moving event types from
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}_{i+1}
directly before
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i}
or directly after
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i+1}
) such that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i+1}
are next to each other.
According to the definition of swarm protocol composition, these two non-interfacing event types from different protocols are concurrent. This is a contradiction.

This means whenever
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i+1}
are from different protocols, then they are interfacing. If
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i}
is non-interfacing, this means
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i-1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i+1}
occur in the same swarm protocol as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i}
and thus they are non-concurrent with each other. This means we can safely remove
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i}
from the sequence
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{1}\ldots{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n-1}
. We can safely remove all non-interfacing event types and then our WLOG assumption holds.

Since
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n-1},\,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n}
occur in the same swarm protocol, say
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{j}
, and has a path
\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n-1}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}_{n-1}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n}}
that means there are
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{j}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n-1}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime\prime}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n}\in{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime\prime}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n}\in{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})
.
It follows
\mathtt{R}\in\mathit{subscribers}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma})
.
Recall, that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{1}\ldots{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n-1}
are interfacing.
The
Interfacing
rule ensures
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n-1}\in{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})
.
We iterate this application of the
Interfacing
rule to get
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{1}\in{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})
.

Assume
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
is not interfacing. This means it occurs only in one swarm protocol
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i}
.
Then
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{1}\in{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})
together with
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{1}
occurs in
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i}
, ensures that
\mathtt{R}\in\mathit{subscribers}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i},)
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
is interfacing, then
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{1}
occur together in some swarm protocol. In this case, the
Interfacing
rule ensures
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})
and thus
\mathtt{R}\in\mathit{subscribers}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i},)
for any
i\leq n
with
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}}
.
∎

Recall that we call those looping event type that satisfies the looping condition in Determinacy and are thus chosen as updating event types
looping in
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
.

Proof of
Theorem

16
.

To prove that
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}={\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}
is
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
-well-formed, we verify the following conditions: (1) confusion-freeness, (2) causal consistency, and (3) determinacy.
It is easy to see that confusion-freeness (
Definition

5
) holds: Property (1) holds since
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}
are confusion-free and interfacing. Properties (2) and (3) hold since the swarm protocol composition preserves these properties.
Causal consistency is enforced by the Causal Consistency rule.
We now verify that determinacy (
Definition

10
) holds by examining each condition that determinacy requires.

Branching.

Assume
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
is branching with
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime}
at
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}
.
It holds
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{n}
where
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i}\sqsubseteq{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i}
for
i\leq n
and there is an
i\leq n
where
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
branches with
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime}
at
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i}
. Thus they are not in
\mathtt{conc}
.
Since
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime}
are not concurrent, but both are outgoing transitions of
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}
,

Definition

2
(Swarm Protocol Composition) ensures there is some
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i}
with
i\leq n
that they both occur in.
For any
\mathtt{R}\in\mathit{roles}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime},)
,
Lemma

39
ensures
\mathtt{R}\in\mathit{subscribers}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i},)
and thus the
Branching
rule sets
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})
.

Joining.

Assume towards contradiction that the Joining condition is not satisfied.
Then there are
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{a},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{b},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{c},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{d}\sqsubseteq{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}
with
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{a}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{b}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{c}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{d}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{b}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b}
are concurrent but not concurrent with
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
and
\mathtt{R}\in\mathit{roles}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{b},)
and
\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\}\not\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})
.
It holds that since
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
are not concurrent, then it is not the case that:
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{a}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{c}
.
According to the composition of swarm protocols, this means that there is a
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i}
and thus
\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\}\not\in\mathtt{conc}
.
Further, there is a
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i}\sqsubseteq{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}}
.
Analogue, we argue there is a
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{j}\sqsubseteq{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{j}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{j}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}}
and thus
\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\}\not\in\mathtt{conc}
.
Since
\mathtt{conc}
is an overapproximation of concurrent event types, it holds
\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b}\}\in\mathtt{conc}
.
This means there is a role
\mathtt{R}\in\mathit{roles}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{b},)
but there is no corresponding
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{j}\sqsubseteq{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{j}
with
j\leq n
,
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{j}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}}
, and
\mathtt{R}\in\mathit{subscribers}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{j},)
. This is a contradiction to
Lemma

39
.

Looping.

Assume towards contradiction that the Looping condition is not satisfied.
Then there is a
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}
with
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}
wit
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}
being non-empty and
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}
has no looping update.
Let
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}=({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{n})
.
According to the composition of swarm protocols, this means that there is an
i\leq n
such that
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i}
and
\epsilon\subset{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}^{\prime}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}
.
If
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}
contains an interface event type, then the interfacing rule ensures that this is a looping update.
If
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}
contains no interface event type, then neither does
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}^{\prime}
.
If
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i}
contains branching event types, then at least one of those event types remains in
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}^{\prime}
, even if it may be no longer branching.
We argued in paragraph
Branching
that the remaining one is in the necessary subscriptions to be a looping update. This is a contradiction.
If
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i}
contains no branching event types, then Step 3 picks an event type
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}^{\prime}
and ensures
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})
for all
\mathtt{R}\in\mathit{subscribers}(,)
.
Since the looping condition is not satisfied by
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
, that means
\mathtt{R}\in\mathit{roles}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime},)
but not
\mathtt{R}\in\mathit{subscribers}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i},)
.
This is a contradiction, the argument is analogue to branching.
∎

Appendix B
Proof of
Lemma 23

We introduce the concept of causal dependency between event types which is implicitly used by the function
\mathit{roles}(,,)
.

Definition 40

(Definition: causal dependency)
.

An event type
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime}
is causally dependent on
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
in if there exists a sequence of transitions

{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{0}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}_{1}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{1}}\ldots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}_{n}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n}}

where

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{0}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime}
, and

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i+1}
are not concurrent for all
i<n
.

A role
\mathtt{R}
is in the set
\mathit{roles}(,,)
if and only if there exists an event
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime}
in that is causally dependent on
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
and that
\mathtt{R}
subscribes to.

Proof of
Lemma

23
by Contradiction

Given a swarm protocol , a subscription
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
such that is well-formed w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
and a role
\mathtt{R}
, we will prove that all projections of onto
\mathtt{R}
for
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
behave identically.

Assume, for contradiction, that there exist two distinct correct projections
\cdot_{1}
and
\cdot_{2}
such that:

A:

\cdot_{1}
and
\cdot_{2}
receive different log types, or

B:

They emit different event types after receiving the same log type.

We analyze these two cases separately.

Case A: Different Log Types.

There is a shortest sequence of event types
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{1},\dots,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
such that:

\displaystyle\cdot_{1}={\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{1}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\ldots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}

\displaystyle\cdot_{2}={\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{1}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\ldots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{n}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}

\displaystyle\not\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}

We show that this is not the case.

Induction Hypothesis:

It holds that for any pairs of sequences

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{1}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\ldots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}

(1)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{1}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\ldots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{n}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}

(2)

there is a
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{n+1}
such that we can append a transition
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{n}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{n+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
to Sequence (
2
).
We apply an induction over the length of the sequences
n
:

Induction Basis
n=0
:

Consider:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{2}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}

It follows immediately that we can append a transition
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{2}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
, since it is the same projection.

Induction Step
(n-1)\rightarrow n
:

It follows from the definition of projections and from the Sequences
1
and
2
, that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{1},\dots,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
is the shortest series of event types such that:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,1}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{a,1}\ldots\ \xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,k}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{a,k}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\ldots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{c,1}\ldots{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{c,l}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{c,l}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n+1}

(3)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b,1}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{b,1}\ldots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b,m}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{b,m}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\dots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{n}

(4)

and the only event types in Sequence
3
and
4
that occur in
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})
are
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{1},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
.

We assume WLOG that Sequence
3
is minimal, meaning the maximal subsequence of Sequence
3
that can be removed while still maintaining a valid path in the swarm protocol with
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
was removed.
For readability, we write Sequence
3
as:

{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,1}}\ldots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,k}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{1}}\ldots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{c,1}}\ldots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{c,l}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}}

We will now show that we can apply a series of changes to Sequence
3
that ensure that it has a prefix that is identical to Sequence
4
.

Let
x
be the smallest index such that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,x}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b,x}
(assuming it exists).

Case A1. There is a
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i,j}
in Sequence
3
such that it is the first occurence of event type
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b,x}
after
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,x}
:

{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,1}}\ldots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,x}}\ldots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i,j}}\ldots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}}

Since
x
is the smallest index where the sequences diverge, it holds
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{a,(x-1)}={\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{b,(x-1)}
.

Argument for Concurrency.

Since both transitions
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,x}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b,x}
are outgoing from
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{a,(x-1)}={\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{b,(x-1)}
and
\mathtt{R}
does not subscribe to at least one of the event types, it follows that either the event types
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,x}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b,x}
are concurrent with each other or they are branching. If they are branching, then determinacy requires that they do not satisfy condition
\mathtt{R}\in\mathit{roles}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,x},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{a,(x-1)},)
of
Definition 10
.Thus,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
is not causally dependent on
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,x}
: There is no subsequence of Sequence
3
starting at
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,x}
and ending at
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
where each event type is non-concurrent with its successor (see definition of
\mathit{roles}(,,)
in
Definition

10
).
This means we can move
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,x}
and all events that are causally dependent on it after
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
.
This means
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,x}
can be removed. This is a contradiction to the sequence being minimal. Thus, the event types are concurrent.

Since
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b,x}
is concurrent with
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,x}
and they are both outgoing from
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{a,(x-1)}={\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{b,(x-1)}
, this means
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{a,(x-1)}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,x}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{a,(x)}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b,x}}
.
Thus,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b,x}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,x+1}
are both outgoing from
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{a,(x)}
. We can apply the Argument for Concurrency, which means they are concurrent.
We iteratively apply this argument to show that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b,x}
is concurrent with every event type between
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,x}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i,j}
in Sequence
3
.
It follows that we can move
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i,j}
directly before
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,x}
and still get a valid interleaving:

{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,1}}\ldots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i,j}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,x}}\ldots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}}

Case A2.
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b,x}
does not occur in Sequence
3
after
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,x}
:

Here, we can apply the Argument for Concurrency from Case A1 and show that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b,x}
is concurrent with every event type after
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,x}
in Sequence
3
.
This means we can append
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b,x}
at the end of the sequence:

{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,1}}\ldots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,x}}\ldots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b,x}}.

Since it is concurrent with
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,x}
and every event type afterwards in Sequence
3
, we can move the appended
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b,x}
directly before
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,x}
.

Conclusion.

Each time we apply this change, the prefix of Sequence
3
that is equal to Sequence
4
gets one transition longer.
We keep applying these changes until
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{a,m}={\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{b,m}
. If no such diverging index existed, then this holds initially and no application were necessary.
If the next transition is
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{a,m}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,m+1}}
, then we move
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{1}
directly before
\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,m+1}}
in Sequence
3
, using the same concurrency argument as above.

We have now adapted Sequence
3
such that it starts with the prefix

{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b,1}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{b,1}\ldots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b,m}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{b,m}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}

followed by a sequence

{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\ldots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{2}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\ldots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime\prime}_{n}\xrightarrow{e}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime\prime}_{n+1}.

Thus, we have constructed a sequence
with a prefix that is equal to Sequence
4
until
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}
.
We remove the prefix and get the following sequences for the series
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{2}\dots{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n}
of length
(n-1)
:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{2}?}\ldots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime\prime}_{n}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime\prime}_{n+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}

(5)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{2}?}\ldots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{n}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}

(6)

We now apply the induction hypothesis to conclude that there is a
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{n+1}
with
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{n}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{n+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
.
Since Sequence
8
also ends in
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{n}
, we can append
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{n}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{n+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
to it:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{1}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\ldots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\;

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}

(7)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{1}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\ldots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{n}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}.

(8)

Case B: Emitting Different Event Types.

We have shown that projections of the same swarm protocol receive the same log types.
It remains to show that they can always emit the same set of event types after receiving the same log type. This is easy to see since the event types a projection can emit are determined by the events from its own role
\mathtt{R}
that it can receive:

Examine
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
from Case A. It holds that
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
emits event type
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
iff there is an
\mathtt{R}\langle{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\rangle
and some sequence

{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,1}}\ldots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,m}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n+1}

where
\mathtt{R}
does not subscribe to any
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,1}\ldots{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{a,m}
.
This is the case iff there is an
\mathtt{R}\langle{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\rangle
and
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}
.
Since
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{n}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
can receive the same event types, it follows
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{n}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}
and thus
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{n}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
can emit
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
.

Appendix C
Formal Definitions of Effective logs

To formally define eventual fidelity, we introduce the concept of
effective global logs
(
Definition

41
),
obtained by filtering out all events
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}
that have the wrong type according to , or have the wrong pointer
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}.\mathsf{last_{up}}
;
to this end,
Definition

41
defines a transition system of a swarm protocol with branch tracking, analogue to the transition systems of machines in
Definition

24
.

Definition 41

(Global effective log)
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}
be of type
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{{}}}.\mathsf{last_{up}}=\mathsf{last_{up}}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}})
, and
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}
. We extend the partial function
\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}}
to swarm protocols as follows:
(1)
if
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
is not updating then
({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}},)\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}}({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}},)
; and
(2)
if
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
is updating, then
({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}},)\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}}({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}},\mathsf{last_{up}}[\mathsf{branch}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}})\mapsto{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}])
.

We define the global effective log filtering function
\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},)
as:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathtt{eff}(\epsilon,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},)&=&\epsilon\\
\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},)&=&\begin{cases}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}\cdot\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime},\mathsf{last_{up}}^{\prime})&\text{if }({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}},)\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}}({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}},\mathsf{last_{up}}^{\prime})\\
\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},)&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\end{array}

Given a log
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}
, we define the
global effective log for
as
\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}):=\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},\emptyset)
.

Similarly to
Definition

41
, the effective log of a machine
\cdot
(
Definition

42
) only includes the events in a log that
\cdot
actually accepts.

Definition 42

(Effective Log of a Machine)
.

Using
\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}}
from
Definition

24
, we define the effective log filtering function
\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}},\cdot,)
for a machine
\cdot
:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathtt{eff}(\epsilon,\cdot,)&=&\epsilon\\
\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}},\cdot,)&=&\begin{cases}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}\cdot\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}},\cdot^{\prime},\mathsf{last_{up}}^{\prime})&\text{if }({},)\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}}({\cdot^{\prime}},\mathsf{last_{up}}^{\prime})\\
\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}},\cdot,)&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\end{array}

Given a log
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}
, we define the
effective log for
\cdot
as
\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}},\cdot):=\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}},\cdot,\emptyset)
.

Appendix D
Proof of Eventual Fidelity

In order to prove eventual fidelity, we require some technical results:
If an event
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}
has type
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
, we say that
\mathtt{R}

subscribes to

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}
if
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})
.

We define
(({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}},),\epsilon):=({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}},)
.
If
({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}},)\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}}({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}},\mathsf{last_{up}}^{\prime})
, then
(({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}},),{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}.{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}):=(({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}},\mathsf{last_{up}}^{\prime}),{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}})
, otherwise
(({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}},),{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}.{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}):=(({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}},),{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}})
.
We set
({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}):=(({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}},\emptyset),{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}})
.

We define the projection of a parameterized swarm protocol
({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}},)
as the projection of the non-parameterized version of the swarm protocol with the same parameter:
({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}},)\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}:=({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}},)
.

We write
({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}},)\sim_{\mathtt{R}}({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}},\mathsf{last_{up}}^{\prime})
if
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}={\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
and for any
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})
holds
\mathsf{last_{up}}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}})=\mathsf{last_{up}}^{\prime}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}})
.
We call event types that are not updating
simple event types
. These are the event types that don’t update the branch tracking function
\mathsf{last_{up}}
.

Lemma 43
.

Let be
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
-well-formed and
\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}})=\mathtt{eff}(,)\downarrow_{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})}
. It follows
({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}})\sim({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
.

Proof.

Let
({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}},\mathsf{last_{up}}^{\prime}):=({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}})
and
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}[\mathsf{last_{up}}^{\prime\prime}]:=({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
.

Lemma

23
ensures
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}={\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
.
Assume towards contradiction that there is an event type
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})
with
\mathsf{last_{up}}^{\prime}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}})\neq\mathsf{last_{up}}^{\prime\prime}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}})
.
Note that the update function for the parameter of a swarm protocol is the same as the update function for the parameter of a projection.
According to
\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}})=\mathtt{eff}(,)\downarrow_{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})}
, any update of the parameter that is performed by the projection is also performed by the swarm protocol.
This means there was an update of the swarm protocol parameter caused by some event
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{b}
of type
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b}
with
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\in\mathsf{branch}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b})
, which was not performed by the projection.
Thus,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{b}
is a branching or joining
event that
\mathtt{R}
does not subscribe to. This means the condition
\mathtt{R}\in\mathit{roles}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{c},)
of
Definition

10
is not satisfied.
Accordingly, it holds that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
, which is in
\mathsf{branch}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b})
, is not subscribed to by
\mathtt{R}
, since that would ensure
\mathtt{R}\in\mathit{roles}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{b},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{c},)
.
∎

Theorem

(Eventual Fidelity -
Theorem

32
)
.

Every realization of a
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
-well-formed swarm protocol is eventually faithful to and
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
.

Proof.

Assume towards contradiction that it does not hold and there is a
({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}},\epsilon)\rightarrow^{*}({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}})
and a longest prefix
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}^{\prime}
with
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}.e.l^{\prime\prime}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}^{\prime}
satisfies eventual fidelity with logs.
This means there is a role
\mathtt{R}
such that
\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}})=\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}})\downarrow_{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})}
but
\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}.e}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}})\neq\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}.e}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}})\downarrow_{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})}
.
Assume
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}
has type
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
.
There are two possibilities:

Case 1:
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}\notin\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}.e}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}}),{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}\in\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}.e}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}})\downarrow_{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})}
.

This means there is a transition
({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{e}}}}{{\rightarrow}}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}.e}})
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})
.
It follows that there is a transition
({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}}})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}}}{{\rightarrow}}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}.e}})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
.
According to
Lemma

43
, it holds
({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}}})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\sim_{\mathtt{R}}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}}})
.
Since
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})
, this means that the function
\mathsf{last_{up}}
of
({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}}})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
assigns the same value to
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
as the corresponding function of
({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}}})
.
From this and
({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}}})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}}}{{\rightarrow}}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}.e}})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
follows
({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}}}{{\rightarrow}}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}.e}})
.
This means
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}\in\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}.e}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}})
which contradicts the assumption of Case 1.

Case 2:
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}\in\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}.e}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}}),{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}\notin\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}.e}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}})\downarrow_{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
is not joining and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}.\mathsf{last_{up}}\neq\mathtt{NULL}
.

Here,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}
is not an interface event that joins multiple concurrent branches together.
Let
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}.\mathsf{last_{up}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{e^{\prime}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}^{\prime\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
Since
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}\in\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}.e}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}})
, we know that when
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}
was received by
\mathtt{R}
, it had the correct pointer value
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}
in
\mathsf{last_{up}}
. This value could only have been added to the function when receiving
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}
.
This means
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}\in\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}.e}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}})
.
From
\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}})=\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}})\downarrow_{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})}
follows
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}\in\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}})
as well.

Let
\mathtt{R}^{\prime}
be the role that emitted
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}
. This role subscribes to any event type that directly precedes
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
non-concurrently according to
Definition

8
.
This means when it emitted
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}
, it had received an event
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{1}
that directly preceded
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}
. That event was either branching or joining (which means it has to be
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}
according to branch tracking) or it was some simple event.
Assume it was a simple event.
According to branch tracking semantics, this ensures
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{1}.\mathsf{last_{up}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}
and thus
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{1}
occurs in
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}}}
after
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}
.
We apply this argument iteratively until we have reached
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}
. This gives us a sequence
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{simple}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{n}\ldots{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{1}
of simple events.
If
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{1}
was not simple, and thus it was
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{simple}}
is the empty sequence.
It holds
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{simple}}
is a subsequence of
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
According to the construction of
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{simple}}
, it holds
({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}^{\prime\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime})\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{n}\ldots{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{1}}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}^{\prime\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{n}\ldots{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{1})
.

Note that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{n}
is a simple event that directly succeeds
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}
.
If
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{n}\notin\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}.e}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}})
, then this means there was an earlier event
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}_{n}
in
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{simple}}
that is a direct successor of
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}
and that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}_{n}
also pointed to
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}
. Since
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{n}
is simple, it holds that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}_{n}
has the same type as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{n}
.
We set
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{n}:={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}_{n}
.
We repeat this step until
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{n}\in\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}.e}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}})
.
We apply this method in sequence to
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{n-1}\ldots{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{1}
as well.
Afterwards,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{simple}}:={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{n}\ldots{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{1}
is a subsequence of
\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}})
that occurs after
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}
.
Let
\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}})={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}_{eff1}.{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{1}.{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}_{eff2}
.
According to the construction of
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{1}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}
is a direct successor of
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{1}
. Since
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}\notin\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}.e}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}})
, it follows that there was another direct successor
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime\prime}
of
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}_{1}
in
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}_{eff2}
that is not concurrent with
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}
.

Case 2A:
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
is simple:

Note that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime\prime}
has the same type
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}
(and thus
\mathtt{R}
subscribes to it) and the same pointer. This means
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}})
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}\in\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}})
.
Since they have the same pointer, that means between receiving
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}
,
\mathtt{R}
only received events that were either simple or concurrent with
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}
. This means there is path
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{s}_{1}\ldots{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{s}_{n}
of simple events types in the swarm protocol such that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{s}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{s}_{n}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
and
n>1
. This is a contradiction to the looping condition of Determinacy.

Case 2B:
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
is branching and not joining:

This means that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime\prime}
can also be another branching option to
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
and thus can be another type then
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}
. Note that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime\prime}
has the same pointer as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}
(and thus
\mathtt{R}
subscribes to it).
This means
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}})
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime\prime}
is not concurrent with
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}
. This means
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}
is not the last branching event that precedes
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}
and thus
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}
pointer to
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}
is not correct. It follows
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}\notin\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}.e}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}})
which is a contradiction to the assumption of Case 2.

Case 2C:
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
is looping in
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
and not joining and not branching:

Note that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime\prime}
has the same (updating) type as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}
and the same pointer as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}
(and thus
\mathtt{R}
subscribes to it).
This means
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}})
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime\prime}
is not concurrent with
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}
. This means
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}
is not the last updating event that precedes
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}
and thus
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}
pointer to
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}
is not correct. It follows
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}\notin\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}.e}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}})
which is a contradiction to the assumption of Case 2.

Case 3:
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}\in\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}.e}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}}),{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}\notin\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}.e}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}})\downarrow_{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
is joining and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}.\mathsf{last_{up}}\neq\mathtt{NULL}
.

It holds
({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}}})\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}}
and
({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}}})\sim_{\mathtt{R}}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}}})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
and thus
({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}}})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}}
.
According to the definition of projections, there is a log
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}
that
\mathtt{R}
does not subscribe to, such that
({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}}})\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}.{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}}
. We assume WLOG that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}
does not contain event types concurrent with
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
.
Here,
\mathtt{R}
subscribes to all event types that directly precede
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
according to
Definition

10
(Joining). This means
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}
is empty and thus
({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}}})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}}
. It follows
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}\in\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}.e}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}})
which is a contradiction to
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}\notin\mathtt{eff}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l^{\prime}.e}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}})\downarrow_{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})}
.

Case 4:
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}.\mathsf{last_{up}}=\mathtt{NULL}
.

This case is analogue to
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}.\mathsf{last_{up}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}
since the cases only argue about the part of the log that follows
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}
. The only difference is that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}^{\prime\prime}
is empty and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}e}^{\prime}
is replaced by a dummy event
\mathtt{NULL}
that everyone received initially.
∎

Appendix E
Swarm Composition Algorithm

We now use
Definition

36
to introduce

Algorithm

3
, which constructs a composed swarm from a set of input swarms
{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{1},\ldots{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{n}
(all having empty logs) by adapting each input swarm machine
\cdot
for the composed swarm protocol
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}
.
First, we obtain the well-formed subscription
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
for the overall composed swarm protocol (line
1
);
Then, traversing the input swarms, we select each machine
\cdot
and its role
\mathtt{R}
(lines
5
and
6
);
finally, we adapt
\cdot
into
\cdot^{\prime}
(line
7
) and we add
\cdot^{\prime}
to the output swarm (line
8
, where the operator
\_\mathbin{{:}{:}}\_
adds
\cdot^{\prime}
to the swarm with a suitable unique index and an empty local log).

Input:

•

Confusion-free, pairwise-interfacing swarm protocols
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\ldots{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}
without concurrent event types;

•

Subscriptions
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}_{1}\ldots{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}_{n}
;

•

Swarms
{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{1}\ldots{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{n}
and mappings
\operatorname{r}_{1}\ldots\operatorname{r}_{n}
such that, for
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
:

–

{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{i}
has empty logs and realises
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i}
for subscriptions
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}_{i}
;

–

\operatorname{r}_{i}\!\left({j}\right)
is the role in
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i}
that is realised by the machine
{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{i}(j)
.

Output:

•

A subscription
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}\supseteq{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}_{i}
(for
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
)
such that
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}
is
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
-well-formed;

•

A swarm that realises
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}
and is constructed by adapting
{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{1}\ldots{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{n}
.

1:

Construct
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
using
Algorithm

1
(by
Theorem

16
).

2:

Let
{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}=\left(\emptyset,\epsilon\right)
.
(Output swarm with no machines and empty global log)

3:

for all

k\in\{1,\ldots,n\}

do

4:

for all

j\in\operatorname{dom}{(}{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{k})

do

5:

\cdot\;:=\;{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{k}(j)

(Select the
j
th
machine
\cdot
from swarm
{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{k}
)

6:

\mathtt{R}\;:=\;\operatorname{r}_{k}\!\left({j}\right)

(Role of machine
\cdot
in swarm protocol
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{k}
)

7:

\cdot^{\prime}\;:=\;\mathcal{A}\!\left(\cdot,{({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i})_{i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}},\mathtt{R}},k,{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}\right)

(Adapt
\cdot
using
Definition

36
)

8:

{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}\;\leftarrow\;\cdot^{\prime}\mathbin{{:}{:}}{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}

(Add adapted machine
\cdot^{\prime}
with empty log to output swarm)

9:

end

for

10:

end

for

Algorithm 3

Swarm Composition.

Appendix F
Proof of
Theorem

38

We show that the constructed swarm is a realization of the composed swarm protocol.
The algorithm adapts each input machine
\cdot={\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{k}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}_{k}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}}
(which is a projection of of some
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{k}
onto a role
\mathtt{R}
) using
Definition

36
.
We will show that each adaptation is a projection of the swarm protocol composition:

\ \mathcal{A}\!\left(\cdot,({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i})_{i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}},\mathtt{R},k,{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}\right)=({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
.

F.1
Technical Requirement

We begin by showing that
\cdot\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{k}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}}={\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{k}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}}
holds:
Since
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}_{k}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
, any event type of
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{k}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}_{k}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}}
also occurs in
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{k}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}}
.
Thus any transition in the composition is either only a transition in
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{k}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}}
or in
\cdot
as well.
Since
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{k}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}_{k}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}}
only differs from
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{k}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}}
by skipping some transitions, it does not restrict the behavior of
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{k}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}}
in the composition.
This means that
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{k}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}}={\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{k}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}_{k}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}}\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{k}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}}
holds.
The condition follows.

F.2
Correct Realisation

The algorithm adapts each each input machine
\cdot={\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{k}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}_{k}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}}
using
Definition

36
.
We will show that each adaptation is a projection of the swarm protocol composition:

\ \mathcal{A}\!\left(\cdot,({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i})_{i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}},\mathtt{R},k,{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}\right)=({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
.

Recall

\mathcal{A}\!\left(\cdot,{({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i})_{i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}},\mathtt{R}},k,{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}\right)\;=\;\left({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}}\mathbin{{\,||\,}}\ldots\mathbin{{\,||\,}}\left(\cdot\mathbin{{\,||\,}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{k}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}}\right)\mathbin{{\,||\,}}\ldots\mathbin{{\,||\,}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}}\right).

We set
\cdot_{k}:=\left(\cdot\mathbin{{\,||\,}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{k}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}}\right)
and
\cdot_{j}:={\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{j}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
for
j\neq k
.
It remains to show that
\cdot_{1}\,||\,\ldots\cdot_{n}=({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
holds, since the condition
\ \mathcal{A}\!\left(\cdot,{({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i})_{i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}},\mathtt{R}},k,{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}\right)=({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
follows immediately.
We use an induction over
i\leq n
:
Let
\cdot_{i}^{\mathtt{R}}:=\cdot_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,\cdot_{i}
for
i\leq n
.

Induction Basis
i=1
:

It holds
\cdot^{\mathtt{R}}_{1}=\cdot_{1}={\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
. This holds either by definition or due to
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}}={\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}_{1}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}}\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}}
for k=1, as shown above.

Induction Hypothesis:

It holds
\cdot_{i}^{\mathtt{R}}=({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
.

Induction Step
i\rightarrow i+1
:

First, we show that for any transition

({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i+1})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i+1})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}},

there is a corresponding transition
\cdot_{i+1}^{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}})
.
According to the composition of swarm protocols, the transition
({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i+1})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i+1})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
satisfies one of these cases:

Case 1 (
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\not\in{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i+1}
):

It holds
({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}

and thus

({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i+1})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}=({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i}\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i+1})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}.

According to the Induction Hypothesis, it holds
\cdot_{i}^{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
.
We apply the machine composition and we derive
\cdot_{i}^{\mathtt{R}}\,||\,({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}})\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
since
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
does not occur in
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
.
It holds
\cdot_{i+1}^{\mathtt{R}}=\cdot_{i}^{\mathtt{R}}\,||\,({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}})
and thus there is a corresponding transition from
\cdot_{i+1}^{\mathtt{R}}
.

Case 2 (
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i+1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\not\in({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i})
):

It holds

({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i+1})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}=({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i}\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i+1})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}.

According to the Induction Hypothesis, it holds
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\not\in\cdot^{\mathtt{R}}_{i}
.
We apply the machine composition (
\cdot^{\mathtt{R}}_{i+1}=\cdot_{i}^{\mathtt{R}}\,||\,({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}})
) and we derive

\cdot_{i}^{\mathtt{R}}\,||\,({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}})\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}.

Case 3 (
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i+1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\in({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i})
):

The event type is interfacing.
According to the definition of swarm protocol composition, it holds
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
as well as

({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}.

We apply the machine composition and we derive
\cdot_{i}^{\mathtt{R}}\,||\,({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}})\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
.

Having shown that for any transition of
({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i+1})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
, there is a corresponding transition in
\cdot_{i+1}^{\mathtt{R}}
,
we will now show that,
for any transition
\cdot_{i+1}^{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}})
, there is a corresponding transition
({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i+1})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i+1})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
.
We examine the different cases for
\cdot_{i+1}^{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}})
:

Case A (
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\not\in\cdot_{i}^{\mathtt{R}}
):

It holds

({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}.

There is a logtype
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}
of minimal length such that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}\cap{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})=\emptyset
and
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i+1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}?}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i+1}
.
Note that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}
does not contain any interfacing event types since
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}\cap{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})=\emptyset
and we constructed
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
in such a way that all interfacing event types are subscribed to if other subscribed event types are causally dependent on them. Since
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}
is minimal, it only contains event types that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
is causally dependent on.
Since
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}
does contains no interfacing event types, it holds
({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i+1})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i}\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i+1})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
.

Case B (
\cdot_{i}^{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\not\in{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
):

This case is analogue to the previous one.

Case C (
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i+1}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
and
\cdot_{i}^{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
):

Note that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
is an interfacing event type.
There are
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}_{2}
such that
({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}_{1}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}_{2})\cap{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}=\emptyset
,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}_{1}\cap{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}_{2}=\emptyset
,
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i+1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}_{1}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i+1}
, and

({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i})\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}_{2}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i}).

Note that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}_{2}
are not subscribed to and we can assume they are minimal. Thus they do not contain any interfacing event types. This means it holds

({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i+1})\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}_{1}.{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}_{2}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i+1})

and thus

({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i+1})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}?}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}_{i+1})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
.

Since the projections have the same transitions and the emitter set is determined by the transitions, the projections emit the same event types. Thus they are equal:

\cdot_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,\cdot_{n}=({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{R}}
. The constructed swarm is almost a realisation of the
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
-well-formed composed protocol
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}
.
The only realisation requirement not met concerns the set of updating events: for each machine
\cdot
,
\cdot.\operatorname{UP}
is an over-approximation (rather than the exact set).
However, this over-approximation is sound for our purposes: for any
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
in that over-approximation, if
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}}
and
\mathtt{R}\in\mathit{roles}(,,)
, then
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R})
.
Moreover, all machines share the same set
\cdot.\operatorname{UP}
.
Therefore, the proof of eventual fidelity in
Appendix

D
still applies under this over-approximation.
Thus,
Theorem

32
can still be applied for the over-approximated set of updating event types and the constructed swarm is eventually faithful to the
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
-well formed composed protocol
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}={\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\ldots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}
.
Thus,
Theorem

38
is proven.

Appendix G
