Inference Rules for Verifying Well-Formedness

We define rules that allow us to check the
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
-well-formedness of a swarm protocol compositions.
To decide whether
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\cdots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}
is
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
-well-formed we rely on a derivation environment consisting of the sets
C
,
S
, and
V
, where:

•

C
is a set of unordered pairs of event types containing an overapproximation of the concurrent event types of
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\cdots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}
.

•

S
is the set of subterms of
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\cdots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}
.

•

V
is a set of subterms of
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\cdots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}
. When we build a derivation bottom-up, we collect in this set the subterms that we have “visisted” and recursively propagate this set between conclusions and premises of the rules.

Furthermore, the inference rules make use of the auxiliary definitions in
Definition

44
below.

Definition 44

(Auxiliary definitions for inference rules.)
.

Phanotom text to force new line. find better solution.

[A.]

1.

The predicate
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\mathsf{composable}}(\{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}\})
is true
iff

\{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}\}
is composable according to
Definition

14
.

2.

For a set of swarm protocols
SP=\{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}\}
, we denote by
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\mathsf{conc}}(SP)
an overapproximation of the set of concurrent event types in
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\cdots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}
given by:

{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\mathsf{conc}}(SP)=\{\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime\prime}\}\;|\;\exists{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{j}\in SP.\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i}\land{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime}\notin{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{j}\land{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{j}\land{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime\prime}\notin{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i}\}

3.

The set of subterms of a swarm protocol
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}=\textstyle\sum_{\in}{\mathtt{}}\langle{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}{{}}}}}\rangle.{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}
, is given by:

{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\mathsf{subterms}}(\textstyle\sum_{\in}{\mathtt{}}\langle{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}{{}}}}}\rangle.{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}})=\{\textstyle\sum_{\in}{\mathtt{}}\langle{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}{{}}}}}\rangle.{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\}\cup\bigcup_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\mathsf{subterms}}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i})

4.

The set of event types branching with
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
at according to a set of concurrent event types
C
is given by:

{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\mathsf{bw}}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},C)=\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime}\;|{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime\prime}\land\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime}\}\notin C\}

5.

The set of event types for which
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
is joining at according to a set of concurrent event types
C
and a set of subterms
S
is given by:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\mathsf{jf}}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},C,S)=\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime}\;|

\displaystyle\;\exists{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{2}\in S.\;\exists{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{2}.\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime\prime}\land{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\land{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{2}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}

\displaystyle\land\{\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime}\},\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime\prime}\}\}\not\subseteq C\land\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime\prime}\}\in C\}

6.

The set of roles that subscribe to event types in that causally depend on
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}
according to the
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
and the set of concurrent event types
C
is given by:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\mathsf{roles}}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma},C)=\{\mathtt{R}\;|\;

\displaystyle\text{there are }n\geq 0,\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{0},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n},\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}_{1},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}_{n}\text{ such that: }

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{0}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}},\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{0}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}_{1}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{1}}\cdots\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}_{n}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n}}\text{ and }

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{n}\in{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R}),\text{ and }\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i+1}\}\notin C\text{ for all }0\leq i<n\}.

Definition 45

(Inference rules for checking well-formedness of swarm protocol compositions)
.

Phanotom text to force new line. find better solution.

We denote by
WF({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\cdots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n})
and
DCC({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\cdots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n})
the sets of subscriptions with respect to which
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\cdots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}
is, respectively, well-formed, and determinate and causal-consistent.
We think of the task of proving that
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\cdots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}
is
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
-well-formed as the same as showing that
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}\in WF({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\cdots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n})
.
To check this, we use the judgment
{}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}\in{\emph{WF}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}||{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{2})}
defined by the following inference rules:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{\emph{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\mathsf{composable}}}(\{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}\})\hskip 8.19447pt{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\mathsf{conc}}(\{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}\}),{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\mathsf{subterms}}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\cdots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n}),\emptyset}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}\in{\emph{DCC}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\cdots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n})}}{{}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}\in{\emph{WF}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{1}\,||\,\cdots\,||\,{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{n})}}\quad\textsc{right={\emph{[Init]}}}\\
\\
\\
\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\in V\hskip 8.19447pt\exists{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}.\;\exists{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}.\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\land{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{l}}\land{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime}}\land\forall\mathtt{R}^{\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\mathsf{roles}}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma},C).\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}^{\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R}^{\prime})}{{C,S,V}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}\in{\emph{DCC}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}})}}\quad\textsc{right={\emph{[Loop]}}}\\
\\
\\
\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}=\textstyle\sum_{\in}{\mathtt{}}\langle{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}{{}}}}}\rangle.{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\quad\forall i\in I.\;\vbox{\hbox{\begin{tabular}[]{l}\phantom{$\land\;\;$}${\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i}\in{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R}_{i})$ \hskip 10.22217pt
$\land\;\;\forall\mathtt{R}^{\prime}\in\{\mathtt{R}\;|\;{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i}\xrightarrow{{\mathtt{\mathtt{R}}}\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}}}}\rangle}\land\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}\}\notin C\}.\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i}\in{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R}^{\prime})$ \hskip 10.22217pt
$\land\;\;{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\mathsf{bw}}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},C)\neq\emptyset\Rightarrow\forall\mathtt{R}^{\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\mathsf{roles}}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma},C).\;{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\mathsf{bw}}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},C)\cup\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i}\}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R}^{\prime})$ \hskip 10.22217pt
$\land\;\;{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\mathsf{jf}}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},C,S)\neq\emptyset\Rightarrow\forall\mathtt{R}^{\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\mathsf{roles}}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma},C).\;{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\mathsf{jf}}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}},C,S)\cup\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{t}}_{i}\}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}(\mathtt{R}^{\prime})$ \hskip 10.22217pt
$\land\;\;{C,S,V\cup\{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}\}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}\in{\emph{DCC}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}_{i})}$\end{tabular}}}}{{C,S,V}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}\in{\emph{DCC}({\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}})}}\quad\textsc{right={\emph{[Term]}}}\end{array}

Appendix H
Non-branch-tracking Swarm

The swarm semantics from
[
41
]
(
Definition

46
below)
are similar to the semantics of
Definition

27
,
but does not involve branch-tracking.
These semantics ensures that a swarm
realising a swarm protocol that is well-formed w.r.t.
a subscription
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
according to the well-formedness
definition from
[
41
]
is eventually faithful
to and
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
.

Definition 46

(Non-Branch-Tracking Swarm Semantics (from
[
41
]
))
.

Given a mapping
m
, let
m[a\mapsto b]
denote the mapping identical to
m
except
m[a\mapsto b](a)=b
.

We write

(\cdot,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}})\xlongrightarrow{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}{{}}}!}(\cdot,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{{}}})
when machine
\cdot
emits an event of type
from state
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}{\delta}}(\cdot,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}})
, thus reaching state

{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}{\delta}}(\cdot,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{{}}})
.
The behaviour of a swarm is defined by
the smallest relation closed under
the following rules:

\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.03749pt]{0.0pt}{11.62495pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}(i)=({}_{i}\cdot{}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}}_{i})\hskip 9.24994pt({}_{i}\cdot{}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}}_{i})\xlongrightarrow{{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}{{}}}}!}({}_{i}\cdot{}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}}_{i}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{{}}})\hskip 9.24994pt<_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}}_{i}}\subseteq<_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}}}}{\textstyle\rule[-1.93748pt]{0.0pt}{11.62495pt}({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}}^{\prime})\xlongrightarrow{{\mathtt{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}{{}}}}!}({{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}}[{i}\mapsto{({}_{i}\cdot{}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}}_{i}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{{}}})}],{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{{}}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}}^{\prime})}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize{\sc[Local]}}\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.03749pt]{0.0pt}{11.62495pt}{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}(i)=({}_{i}\cdot{}_{i},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}}_{i})\hskip 9.24994pt<_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}}_{i}}\subset<_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}}^{\prime}}\subseteq<_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}}}}{\textstyle\rule[-1.93748pt]{0.0pt}{11.62495pt}({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}})\xlongrightarrow{\tau}({{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}}[{i}\mapsto{({}_{i}\cdot{}_{i},{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}}^{\prime}})}],{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}})}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize{\sc[Prop]}}\end{array}

Well-formedness as defined in
Definition

12
, however,
necessitates branch-tracking to ensure eventual fidelity.
Using well-formedness as defined in
Definition

12
together
with the swarm semantics in
Definition

46
,
leads to swarm behaviours where machines can enter
conflicting states, from where they cannot recover consistency.
This is shown in the following example.

Example 47

(Incompatibilty between well-formedness in
Definition

12
and swarm semantics in
[
41
]
)
.

Consider
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{Warehouse}}
in
Figure

1
.
Let
E
denote the set of all event types in
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{Warehouse}}
and let
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}=\{\mathtt{T}\mapsto E,\mathtt{FL}\mapsto E\setminus\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{partOK}}\},\mathtt{D}\mapsto E\setminus\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{pos}}\}\}
.
The projections are given in
Figure

11
.
By
Definition

12
,
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{Warehouse}}
is
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
-well-formed. Consider swarm
{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{W}}
with:

{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{W}}\;=\;\left\{i_{{\mathtt{T}}_{1}}\mapsto({}_{\mathtt{T}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{T}},\epsilon),\quad i_{{\mathtt{T}}_{2}}\mapsto({}_{\mathtt{T}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{T}},\epsilon),\quad i_{{\mathtt{FL}}}\mapsto({}_{\mathtt{FL}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{FL}},\epsilon),\quad i_{{\mathtt{D}}}\mapsto({}_{\mathtt{D}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{D}},\epsilon)\right\}

where
{}_{\mathtt{T}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{T}}={\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{Warehouse}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{T}}}
,
{}_{\mathtt{FL}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{FL}}={\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{Warehouse}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{FL}}}
,
and
{}_{\mathtt{D}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{D}}={\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{Warehouse}}\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{D}}}
. Observe that the machines with IDs
i_{{\mathtt{T}}_{1}}
and
i_{{\mathtt{T}}_{2}}
both enact role
\mathtt{T}
(transport).
Since all machines are obtained by projecting
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{Warehouse}}
onto its roles using
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
, the swarm
{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{W}}
realises the protocol
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{Warehouse}}
w.r.t.
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
(as defined in
[
41
]
).
A possible execution of
{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{W}}
by
Definition

46
(from
[
41
]
) is:

\begin{array}[]{r@{\;}r@{\;}l@{\quad}c@{\;}r@{\;}c@{\;}l}&({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{W}},\epsilon)\xlongrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{partReq}}}!}&({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{1})&&{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{1}&=&{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{W}}{[i_{{\mathtt{T}}_{1}}\mapsto({}_{\mathtt{T}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{T}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{1})]}\\
&\xlongrightarrow{\tau}&({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{1})&&{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{2}&=&{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{1}{[i_{{\mathtt{FL}}_{1}}\mapsto({}_{\mathtt{FL}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{FL}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{1})]}\\
&\xlongrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{pos}}}!}&({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{3},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{1}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{pos}}_{2})&&{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{3}&=&{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{2}{[i_{\mathtt{FL}}\mapsto({}_{\mathtt{FL}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{FL}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{1}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{pos}}_{2})]}\\
&\xlongrightarrow{\tau}&({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{4},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{1}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{pos}}_{2})&&{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{4}&=&{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{3}{[i_{{\mathtt{T}}_{1}}\mapsto({}_{\mathtt{T}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{T}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{1}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{pos}}_{2})]}\\
&\xlongrightarrow{\tau}&({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{5},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{1}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{pos}}_{2})&&{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{5}&=&{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{4}{[i_{{\mathtt{T}}_{2}}\mapsto({}_{\mathtt{T}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{T}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{1}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{pos}}_{2})]}\\
&\xlongrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{partOK}}}!}&({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{6},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{1}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{pos}}_{2}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partOK}}_{3})&&{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{6}&=&{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{5}{[i_{{\mathtt{T}}_{1}}\mapsto({}_{\mathtt{T}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{T}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{1}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{pos}}_{2}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partOK}}_{3})]}\\
&\xlongrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{partReq}}}!}&({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{7},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{1}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{pos}}_{2}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partOK}}_{3}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{4})&&{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{7}&=&{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{6}{[i_{{\mathtt{T}}_{1}}\mapsto({}_{\mathtt{T}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{T}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{1}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{pos}}_{2}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partOK}}_{3}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{4})]}\\
&\xlongrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{partOK}}}!}&({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{8},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{1}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{pos}}_{2}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partOK}}_{3}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{4}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partOK}}_{5})&&{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{8}&=&{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{7}{[i_{{\mathtt{T}}_{2}}\mapsto({}_{\mathtt{T}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{T}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{1}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{pos}}_{2}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partOK}}_{5})]}\\
&\xlongrightarrow{\tau}&({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{9},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{1}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{pos}}_{2}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partOK}}_{3}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{4}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partOK}}_{5})&&{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{9}&=&{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{8}{[i_{{\mathtt{D}}}\mapsto({}_{\mathtt{D}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{D}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{1}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{pos}}_{2}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partOK}}_{3}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{4}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partOK}}_{5})]}\\
&\xlongrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{closingTime}}}!}&({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{10},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}})&&{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{10}&=&{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{9}{[i_{{\mathtt{D}}}\mapsto({}_{\mathtt{D}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{D}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}})]}\\
&\xlongrightarrow{\tau}^{*}&({{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{11},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}})&&{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{11}&=&{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{10}{[i_{{\mathtt{T}}_{1}}\mapsto({}_{\mathtt{T}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{T}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}}),i_{{\mathtt{T}}_{2}}\mapsto({}_{\mathtt{T}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{T}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}}),i_{{\mathtt{FL}}}\mapsto({}_{\mathtt{FL}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{FL}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}})]}\\
\end{array}

where the global log
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{1}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{pos}}_{2}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partOK}}_{3}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{4}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partOK}}_{5}\logcat{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{closingTime}}_{6}

has propagated to every machine. (Recall that by
Definition

46
,
\tau
-transitions
denote log propagation.)

Observe that in state
{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{5}
, the machine with ID
i_{{\mathtt{T}}_{1}}
emits an event
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partOK}}_{3}
as a response to
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{1}
;
however, the event does not immediately propagate to machine
i_{{\mathtt{T}}_{2}}
, which (in state
{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{7}
) responds to
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{1}
with another event
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partOK}}_{5}
.
As the log propagates to
i_{{\mathtt{T}}_{2}}
(in state
{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{11}
), the machine sees that its event
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partOK}}_{5}
is invalidated by the earlier
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partOK}}_{3}
.

However, the machine
i_{\mathtt{D}}
(for the door) reaches a different conclusion: based on its local log in state
{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{9}
, it interprets the event
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partOK}}_{5}
as a response to

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{4}
, when it was in fact emitted as a response to
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{partReq}}_{1}
. Consequently, the machines in do not reach a consensus on which events are valid and conform to
an execution of
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{Warehouse}}
— despite having been projected from
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{Warehouse}}
for
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
. Specifically, in the final swarm state
{{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{}}}_{11}
reached after the propagation of the log
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{l}}
,
the two machines
{}_{\mathtt{T}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{T}}
(for the transport) with IDs
i_{{\mathtt{T}}_{1}}
and
i_{{\mathtt{T}}_{2}}
agree on the projection of the same state of the swarm protocol
{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{Warehouse}}

— whereas
{}_{\mathtt{D}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{D}}
with ID
i_{\mathtt{D}}
is in a completely different state:

{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}{\delta}}({}_{\mathtt{T}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{T}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}})\;=\;({\mathtt{FL}}\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{pos}}}\rangle.{\mathtt{T}}\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{partOK}}}\rangle.{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}\mathsf{Warehouse}})\mathbin{\,\downarrow}^{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}}_{\mathtt{\mathtt{T}}}\quad\text{and}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}{\delta}}({}_{\mathtt{D}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{D}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathit{}})\;=\;{{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\mathbf{0}}}

The machines
{}_{\mathtt{T}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{T}}
expect the forklift to emit an event of type
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{pos}}
, while
{}_{\mathtt{D}}\cdot{}_{\mathtt{D}}
believes
the door has closed and the protocol has ended.
The swarm cannot recover from this conflict.

12

To avoid the issue in
Example

47
with the swarm semantics
Definition

46
(from
[
41
]
),
we should also subscribe role
\mathtt{D}
in
{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.4,0.3}\sigma}
to events of type
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.2}\mathtt{pos}}
. The additional subscriptions needed by Def.
46
would increase with swarm composition,
leading to inefficient swarms: we measure these additional subscriptions in § 6.1.
